Nobody dies of an illness and nobody catches it. On the game test server illnesses by chance are off; the tests make them themselves. The "Citizens" tab shows who is ill, where they are treated and how far they got.
- **Festivals.** The king or a member with the right `manage_citizens` (the right of the professions of citizens: a festival is a matter of the citizens) calls a festival in the "Citizens" tab, view "life": the treasury pays `festivalPerCitizen` for every citizen, the storehouses give a treat of `festivalFood` food points per citizen (without the treat a festival gives half the mood), at most once in `festivalEveryDays` days. From noon the citizens rest and gather at the throne or the town hall of their city, the mood `festivalMood` holds for `festivalMoodDays` days, the chronicle of festivals records it. Once a year the day the kingdom was founded is a festival by itself (a year is `daysPerYear` = 96 game days, twelve turns of the moon of 8 days), free, with a treat if there is one.
- **Fairs.** Every `fairEveryDays` days every city of level `fairLevel` (large by default) with a working market holds a fair on a day of its own: the merchant posts of the city take `fairSeats` merchants more, the market nearby sells `fairDiscount` permille cheaper, and the merchants of every kingdom that has a deal with the host and a way partly by road bring up to `fairVisitorItems` items of their export surplus from their storehouses and put them up as lots of their kingdom. The takings go to the merchants' treasury (into the reserves of the host's currency), what is left goes back to their storehouses the next day. Members of the kingdom and players on its land get a message.
- **On the atlas** a festival is a flag in the colour of the kingdom over the capital, a fair a striped awning over the city. The "Citizens" tab, view "life": how many citizens sleep, work, sit in the tavern and rest now, the day by the hour, taverns and the guests of the evening, the festival (today, how long ago, when one may be called and what it costs, the button), the fairs of the cities, the ill and the chronicle of festivals and fairs.
- **Load.** No new scans of the world every tick: the goal of the day thinks once a second, finds taverns in the register of buildings and takes paths from the shared budget; festivals, fairs and rooms are looked at every 5 seconds, rumors are made once a game day per tavern.

### Diplomacy and war

- **Relations.** Every pair of kingdoms (of players and of the world) has a relation from -100 to 100 with its reasons (treaties, the alliance, help and refusal in war, broken word, declined offers, war, peace). It cools a little every day. A ruler of the world adds its own memory of the neighbour (deals, roads, land).
- **Bonds.** Peace (the default), a pact of non-aggression for `pactDays` days, a defensive alliance, vassalage (the vassal pays its overlord `vassalPermille` permille of the income of the day before: taxes, trade, the law, taverns; it declares no wars of its own; the overlord joins its wars), war (from the declaration) and a truce of `truceDays` days after a war (no war can be declared, it cannot be broken).
- **Offers** work like deals of trade: a pact, an alliance, vassalage (become a vassal or demand fealty), freedom of a vassal, peace on terms. They wait `proposalMinutes` minutes for an answer. The right `manage_diplomacy` belongs to the king and the chancellor (old worlds get it once, upgrade `diplomacy_rights`). Breaking a pact or an alliance costs the relation with the victim and with everybody who knows either of them (chronicle, rumors). An overlord may let a vassal go; a vassal leaves in peace with consent or by a war for independence.
- **Declaring war** (right `declare_war`, the king alone by default): the goal is tribute (vassalage, or `tributePermille` permille of income for `tributeDays` days), an indemnity (`indemnityPermille` permille of the treasury), a raid, land, a city or the throne (taken in the world by the next stage; the terms of peace record the claims). The war starts after `preparationMinutes`; both sides are told, and it shows in the tab, on the atlas and in the chronicle. The allies of the defender are called and answer for themselves (a refusal costs the relation); vassals and the overlord join at once. Not allowed: during a truce, during a pact or an alliance without breaking it (breaking it for war gives the enemy +15 of score), on one's own vassal or overlord (except a war for independence), on a kingdom of players with nobody online.
- **Score and exhaustion** of each side go from 0 to 100. Score comes from battles of squads won (8) and soldiers put down (1), enemies online killed in PvP (5), caravans robbed (6) and deals of the enemy blocked (3); holding the goal comes with the next stage. Exhaustion grows by `exhaustionPerDay` a day, faster with an empty treasury, and with losses and robbed caravans. A side worn out to 100 makes peace on the terms of the enemy by itself (paying what it has). After `maxWarDays` days a war ends in a white peace.
- **Peace.** The leading kingdoms offer terms by their goals: with a lead in score of `imposeLead` a side may ask its goal in full, with a smaller lead half of it (vassalage becomes a tribute), without a lead only a white peace; giving in is always possible. Terms apply together: the indemnity moves from the treasury of the loser into the reserves of the winner by the books (ledger line "War"), then the truce, vassalage, tribute and the claims of land, a city and the throne. If the loser cannot pay, there is no peace.
- **War far from players.** Squads ordered to hold, guard or patrol a place on the land of the enemy advance: every `stepSeconds`, with the chance `battleChance`, they meet a squad of the enemy that holds its land or stays home and fight by the battles of the army (losses, wounds, experience and wear as in the army). Without defenders a squad ravages the land. A squad in the field falls upon a caravan of the enemy with the chance `raidChance` on the mechanics of the ambushes of robbers: the guards fight, and if they lose a share of the cargo goes to the storehouses of the raiders (what does not fit is left on the road) and the payment for it back to the buyer. Deals between enemies are frozen, and a caravan of an enemy does not pass the land of a kingdom at war with it (a blocked delivery is no failure of the deal).
- **Near players.** Soldiers and guards of kingdoms at war fight enemy soldiers and enemy players online at the keyboard. Players of kingdoms at war may fight each other; players of kingdoms at peace may not (`protectPeacefulPlayers`; it does not protect the wanted). Citizens of the enemy may be hurt. Neither the law of the enemy nor one's own law counts blows and killings of an enemy as crimes; theft and the land stay protected as in peace (sieges and captures come with the next stage). Entering the land of an enemy shows a line above the hotbar.
- **Protection of the absent.** Players who are away or offline are hurt neither by soldiers nor by players. A kingdom of players with nobody online at the keyboard is shielded `absenceMinutes` minutes after the last one left (and not sooner than `fightGraceSeconds` after a fight near its players): no battles of squads on its land, its caravans are not robbed, no score grows against it and it does not tire. The atlas and the tab show it. Kingdoms of the world have no shield, their army defends them.
- **Rulers of the world.** A merchant looks for pacts and alliances, a builder for pacts, a warrior declares war on a weak neighbour (`npcStrength` times weaker) for tribute or loot, not more often than every `npcWarDays` days and not on a kingdom just out of a war; a just ruler never attacks first but stands by its allies; a mage keeps out. They answer offers by character and relation (with the reason of a refusal), accept peace when tired or beaten, and offer peace or press their terms when far ahead. Wars on players come at most once every `npcPlayerWarDays` days per kingdom and only with `npcWarsOnPlayers`. Wars of kingdoms of the world go on abstractly and show in the chronicle, the rumors and on the atlas.
- **The "Diplomacy" tab** of the throne: kingdoms with relation and bond (actions: pact, alliance, vassalage, freedom, breaking, the goal and the declaration of war), offers and calls with their answers, wars (goal, time, score, exhaustion, allies, the terms that may be asked or given, buttons of peace), the chronicle. The atlas: a bar in the colour of the relation under the crown of a capital, a sign of war, alliance, pact, vassalage and truce, a blue frame around a shielded kingdom; the places of battles go to the "battles" layer of map mods. Taverns tell of wars, battles, raids and peace.
- **Load.** Diplomacy is counted once a second, a step of the wars every `stepSeconds`, the decisions of rulers once a game day; there are no new scans of the world (soldiers look for enemies only while their kingdom is at war).

### War in the world (sieges, destruction, capture)

- **Open land.** The land of a kingdom is open to the players of the other side only while the war goes on (not in preparation, not in a truce) and the kingdom is not shielded for its absent players (a kingdom of players has somebody online at the keyboard; kingdoms of the world have no shield, their army keeps them). Land of third kingdoms and unowned land are never touched. When the defenders leave, the shield comes by the rules of 9a and the siege stands still.
- **Destruction and loot.** On open land the enemy breaks and places blocks and opens chests and storehouses (loot of war, a little score for every chest); the law does not judge it. The throne and blocks of the tag `kingdomworld:unclearable` never break. Explosions break blocks on open land (`siege.explosions`), never on land of third kingdoms or under the shield. Blocks of registered buildings and walls broken in war are written down: after the war (or sooner, when no enemy came for `siege.repairQuietMinutes` minutes) builders put them back, paid with items from the storehouses. A ruler of the world orders what is missing from its workshops; a player sees in the tab what is missing. Buildings outside the register are not mended: the hint of the declaration of war and the tab warn of it.
- **Gates.** Gates of walls get leaves: fence gates (planks) on a palisade, an iron portcullis on a stone wall, four rows in the opening. Builders hang them from the storehouses (12 fence gates or 48 planks, 12 iron bars or 5 ingots) at the first check, on walls of old worlds too. In war, with an enemy within 24 blocks, gates close by themselves; they open for subjects and allies (within 4 blocks, while no enemy is close); an enemy cannot open them, only break them. Strength: planks `siege.woodGateStrength` (720), iron `siege.ironGateStrength` (1800). A soldier with the order "siege" at a gate takes 1 a second (six soldiers at most), a ram 3 (two at most): a squad with a ram breaks a palisade gate in 80 seconds and a portcullis in 3-4 minutes. A player with a ram in hand breaks leaves and walls faster. Broken leaves fall as items, the gate stands open until new ones are hung.
- **Ram** (recipe: two logs and a block of iron in a row). In the hands of a player it breaks leaves, logs and stone of walls faster; a siege brings rams (an operator command, or a ruler of the world builds them from two logs and nine iron ingots).
- **Orders of the army.** "Siege" (the marshal's baton in siege mode on a city of the enemy within 160 blocks): the squad goes to the nearest closed gate, strikes it, then to the square. "Defend" (the baton on an own city): the squad stands at the city. The marshal's right, like other orders.
- **Holds.** A chunk of the enemy is occupied when attackers (players online or soldiers) are on it for `siege.occupyMinutes` minutes (3) without defenders; defenders free it by holding it as long. A city is taken when attackers hold the square at the town hall (12 blocks) for `siege.plazaMinutes` minutes (5) without defenders. The throne is taken when the throne hall (8 blocks from the throne) is held for `siege.throneMinutes` minutes and the player king of the defenders was online in this war. Defenders coming interrupt the hold (the time starts over); under the shield of the absent it stands still. Taking gives score. Far from players squads count: a besieging squad strikes the gate by half its strength and after the breach holds the square while no squad of the defenders defends, holds or guards there.
- **Peace.** The goal "land": the chunks occupied by the winner go to it (no more than the terms, never the capital chunk), the others come back. "A city": the taken city with its land around (64 blocks), buildings, citizens (soldiers excepted, their mood falls) and wall goes to the winner. "The throne": an annexation: the treasury (at its worth in the currency of the winner, reserves as they are), land, cities (the capital becomes a city of the winner), buildings and citizens go to the winner; players of the loser choose in the chat "Become a subject" or "Stay free" (`/kingdom conquest swear|free`, three days), the former king loses the crown and the chronicle tells it. With nothing taken the peace hands nothing over in the world. Everything goes by the steps of a journal: a restart in the middle neither loses nor doubles anything.
- **Bodies.** Near players soldiers of both sides fight as bodies (the goals of the army of 9a); one siege has at most `siege.maxBodies` bodies (60) per kingdom, the rest fight far from players.
- **Rulers of the world.** A warrior at war for a city or the throne besieges the nearest city of the enemy (or the capital) when its land is open: it builds a ram and sends half its squads; one siege at a time. A ruler whose city is besieged sends squads to defend it. The switch `npcWarsOnPlayers` of 9a holds.
- **Visibility.** The line above the hotbar: "Siege: gate 40%", "Holding the square: 3 of 5 minutes". The "Diplomacy" tab, mode "In the world": sieges, holds, occupied chunks, what peaces hand over, what is broken and what mending takes, what is missing, the warning about buildings outside the register. The atlas: occupied chunks hatched, a besieged city in a ring with the share of its gate broken, a taken city with a banner, details on hover. Chronicle and rumors: sieges, broken gates, captures, annexations, lost crowns.
- **Commands.** `/kingdomworld admin siege status|unlock|timing`, `start <attacker> <defender> [city] [rams]`, `stop`, `gate <kingdom> hang|break|list`, `hold <attacker> <defender> city|throne [city] [percent]`, `occupy <attacker> <defender>` (the chunk of the source), `annex <winner> <loser>`, `cede <winner> <loser> <city>`, `repair <kingdom> [blocks]`, `count <kingdom>`.
- **Data** in `kingdomworld_siege.dat` (format 1): gates, sieges, holds, the journal of conquests, war damage, which kings were online, the choices of players of annexed kingdoms. Data that cannot be read keeps sieges read-only.
- **Load.** Sieges are counted once a second; the game test measure: 30 against 30 soldiers as bodies next to one another, an average tick of 6 ms, the worst 23 ms.

### Nobility, factions, intrigue and espionage

- **Houses.** A noble house: a family (its name in both alphabets, quoted in Russian lines), a head with a character and a trait, a party, wealth and loyalty to the crown (0-100). A kingdom of the world has 2-5 houses from the first day of its court, with titles on its cities (counts) and marches (barons); the first house of a large kingdom is a duke. A kingdom of players has no houses until its king grants a title. Houses have no bodies; heads grow old and die, and the heir takes over.
- **Titles.** Baron, count, duke; a title always comes with a fief: a city of the kingdom or one of its four marches (the land of the kingdom north, east, south or west of the capital outside its cities). The holder gets a share of the income of the fief every day (`nobility.shareBaron` 10%, `shareCount` 15%, `shareDuke` 20% of yesterday's income, in the part of the citizens who live there): a player onto the account, a house into its wealth; all by the books (`Nobility` in the treasury journal). The king and the chancellor (right `manage_nobility`) grant a title to a house, a new house or a subject, take it back and hand it over in the Nobility tab. The law does not judge a noble player for theft, the large cities of the kingdom let them in free, and they get no rights over the treasury (like the honorary councillors of 8b). A ruler of the world makes a player a baron when they dealt well with it and have a high reputation in its land, at most once in 7 days.
- **Stability** 0-100: a base of 50, the loyalty of the houses, the mood of the citizens, the tax against the usual one, the weariness of war, defeats and lost cities of the last 10 days, a recent festival, the content of the parties and what happened lately (a refused demand, incitement, an execution, mercy, rebels, a city that left). The tab shows the causes. Low stability (below `nobility.plotBelow`, 30) spoils the mood of the citizens (they work and grow worse), high stability (from 70) cheers them.
- **Parties:** the war party, the merchants, the old nobility, the crown party. Houses join by their character; the influence of a party grows with its houses, their titles and wealth. At most once in `nobility.demandEveryDays` (3) played days the least content party demands: a lower tax, a war or a peace, a title, works, a share of the loot. The king answers with a button: agree (it is done and costs money), refuse (content and stability fall), bargain (the house gets half the price, half the offence). A demand not answered in `demandDueDays` (2) played days counts as a mild refusal. Rulers of the world answer by their traits and never start a war or make peace because a party asks.
- **A played day** of a kingdom of players is a game day on which one of its players was online. Demands, plots and rebels move only on such days: a player who comes back finds no catastrophe.
- **Plots.** When the stability stays below 30 for two played days, a discontented house (loyalty below 40) may start a plot; it ripens in `nobility.plotDays` (4) played days. Counter-intelligence (15% base, plus spies hunting foreign agents and an appointed spymaster) may find it out every day; then the king decides the fate of the house (execute the head, pardon, exile). A plot against a ruler of the world makes the head of the house the new ruler with their character (chronicle, rumors, the kingdom stays the same). Against a king of players a plot never takes the crown: rebels keep `rebelPercent` (25%) of the income for `rebelDays` (4) played days; only after long unrest (`secessionDays`, 6 played days of low stability) and a warning (after `secessionWarnDays`, 3) the city of the house leaves the kingdom: to the neighbour who backed the plot by incitement, or as a small kingdom of the world with the head of the house on its throne. The city goes through the journal of conquests of 9b (land, buildings, citizens, walls) without loss or duplication; it can be won back by war or diplomacy.
- **Spies.** The spymaster (right `manage_spies`, the king holds it too) hires spies among the agents of the guild (`nobility.spyHire` 100, a wage of `spyWage` 5 a day, at most `maxSpies` 4). Missions in another kingdom: scouting (treasury, soldiers, wars, the plans of the ruler, whether a plot brews), incitement (less stability; a plot there ripens faster and we back it), sabotage (food in the storehouses spoils), theft (5% of the treasury, at most 400, from their treasury into ours by the books), the murder of the head of a house (kingdoms of the world only). Success depends on the skill of the spy, the counter-intelligence of the target and the relations; a failure brings the spy back empty-handed or gets them caught (a ransom of `ransom` 150 within `ransomDays` 3 days, else death; proud and bold rulers of the world execute at once). A caught spy and a backed plot spoil the relations (reasons "their spy caught", "they backed a plot"), a reason for war. Missions against a kingdom of players with nobody online wait at its border. Rulers of the world keep 0-2 spies by their character and send them against enemies and neighbours they dislike.
- **Marriages of houses** of two kingdoms on good terms (chance `marriageChance` a day) raise the loyalty of the houses and the relations of the kingdoms (reason "a marriage of houses").
- **Visibility.** The throne tab "Nobility": houses (head, party, loyalty; titles and wealth on hover) with the choice of title and fief to grant; titles with fiefs and holders; the court (stability with its causes, parties, the demand with its buttons, plots found out with verdicts); the chronicle; for the secret service the spies (skill, mission, captivity), the choice of task and target, and reports. Messages of demands, plots found out, the warning of a city that may leave, rebels, the share of a fief. Rumors of taverns: marriages and deaths of the great, caught spies, hints of plots.
- **Commands.** `/kingdomworld admin nobility status|unlock|tick|day|timing`, `house <kingdom> [add]`, `house loyalty <house> <0-100>`, `title house|player <kingdom> <house|player> <baron|count|duke> <fief>` (fief `c<city id>` or `m<0-3>`), `title revoke <title>`, `stability <kingdom> [low <days>] [<cause> <amount> <days>]`, `faction <kingdom> <party> <content>`, `demand <house> <kind> [value]`, `demand answer <kingdom> accept|refuse|bargain`, `plot <house> <progress> [backer]`, `plot discover|verdict <kingdom> ...`, `spy list|hire|send|ransom ...`.
- **Data** in `kingdomworld_nobility.dat` (format 1): houses, titles, courts (content of parties, played days, days of unrest, recent events, chronicle, reports), demands, plots, spies. Data that cannot be understood keeps the nobility read-only. Old worlds get the rights `manage_nobility` (chancellor) and `manage_spies` (spymaster) with the upgrade `nobility_rights`.
- **Load.** The nobility moves once a game day: one kingdom a tick, without scanning the world, then the spies and the marriages.

### Installing on a server (exaroton)

- Put `kingdomworld-1.0.0.jar` into the `mods` folder of the server and into `mods` of every player: the server does not let in a client without the mod (or with another version), Forge lists the mods that do not match.
- Memory: 8-10 GB for a server with the owner's pack (61 mods); the mod itself takes some tens of megabytes in the measurements (see "Performance"). A server without other mods does with 3-4 GB.
- Settings of the world: `world/serverconfig/kingdomworld-server.toml` (made on the first start; change it while the server is stopped, or with `/reload` for some values). Common: `config/kingdomworld-common.toml`, a player's: `config/kingdomworld-client.toml`.
- What is usually changed:
  - `[war] npcWarsOnPlayers = false`: rulers of the world declare no wars on kingdoms of players (by default they do, rarely);
  - `[war] protectPeacefulPlayers`: players of kingdoms at peace cannot hurt each other;
  - `[siege] explosions = false`: explosions break no blocks even on open land of an enemy;
  - `[npcKingdoms]`: how many kingdoms of the world the plan has and how far apart, how they appear as players come near;
  - `[nobility] maxHouses`, `demandEveryDays`, `secessionDays`: how much nobility there is, how often it demands, after how many days of unrest a city may leave;
  - `[economy] wages`: the wages of professions (new defaults go only into a new config file).
- Backups: the data of the mod lies in `world/data/kingdomworld_*.dat`; copy it with the world.
- Measuring the load of a live server: `/kingdomworld admin perf reset`, after half an hour of play `/kingdomworld admin perf report`.

### Requirements

- Minecraft 1.20.1
- Forge 47.4.10 or newer in the 47.x line
- Java 17
- Install the mod on both the server and the client. Versions must match, otherwise the connection is refused.
- No other mods are required. Integrations with other mods (Create, map mods and so on) switch on automatically, only when that mod is installed.

The mod is built for dedicated servers: all logic and every state change run on the server only; the client only displays data and sends intents that the server validates (rights, distance to the throne, request size and rate limits).

### Building

```
gradlew build
```

The jar ends up in `build/libs/kingdomworld-<version>.jar`. JDK 17 is required; if the system `java` is a different version, set `JAVA_HOME` first (see the Russian section for an example). The first build downloads and prepares Minecraft, which takes a few minutes.

### Tests

| What | Command |
|---|---|
| Core unit tests and architecture checks | `gradlew test` (part of `gradlew build`) |
| Forge GameTests | `gradlew runGameTestServer` |
| Smoke test on a clean dedicated server | `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/smoke-server.ps1` |
| Smoke test with the modpack's mods | `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/smoke-server.ps1 -Pack` |

Core unit tests cover claiming and giving up chunks (connectivity, limits, distance to other kingdoms), rights of positions, membership transitions and the access policy, and citizens: names in both alphabets, beds and workplaces, death and the chronicle, growth, food, mood, the profession registry and the choice of citizens with a body. The translation test checks that `en_us.json` and `ru_ru.json` hold the same keys with non-empty values and the same number of arguments, that Russian values are written in Russian, and that every key the code uses exists. All text a player sees goes through translation keys. Production unit tests check the choice of recipes, tag ingredients, sub-orders, loops and the depth limit of chains, reservations, keep quotas and the formulas of the workshops. Money unit tests check item values (recipes, tags as choices, cycles, the default), reading amounts, coins and change, the journal of the treasury, payments from the treasury with change, wages when money is short, taxes and growing debts, the Treasury right, old currencies, overflows and the plural forms of Russian and English. The architecture tests check that the `core` package does not depend on Minecraft, Forge or Mojang, that client code is only reached through guarded entry points and that compat modules are not referenced directly. Trade unit tests check the price curve and the spread, that exporting is always cheaper than importing, the tax on sales, rates and their smoothing, that exchanges neither make nor lose money (a property test over random exchanges), escrow of deals and lost caravans, imports within the daily limit, lots, the rights of the market and of deals, paying with coins and change, and the plural forms of items, days and minutes. Guild unit tests check the ranks of monsters from their attributes, bosses and overrides, the chance of crystals and the guard against farms and spawners, joining and points, exams and the wait after a failed one, the life of a contract and its escrow (payment less the commission, the escrow back when a contract runs out or is called off, lost points, the posting fee, the daily budget of the guild), handing in items, squads and how expeditions end with wounds and deaths through `TaskRisks`; the translation test checks the plural forms of monsters, bosses, points and crystals. Construction unit tests check turning and mirroring of positions and directions (together, the way the game does), the order of work (clearing from the top down, then full and leaning blocks from the bottom up), the cost of templates (doors, beds, double slabs, candles, liquids, blocks without an item), the state machine of constructions with their rights and limits, work far from players (a block paid whole, time carried over, experience) and the plural forms of blocks; another test checks that the built-in templates in the resources match what the generator draws, byte for byte. Unit tests of buildings check the open register of roles (the mod's roles, roles of code and of data packs, code winning), the numbers of roles by level and a template's own numbers, the threshold of integrity and the states of a role (works, damaged, demolished, off our land, none), the role coming back after a repair, comfort and growth with beds in houses, the difference of templates for an upgrade (the same untouched, the rest taken out, only the new paid), the plan and cost of a repair, what a demolition gives back with the loss (block by block as much as in one go) and its order, the table of woods (the game's woods and those of an invented mod, nether stems and hyphae, a block a species lacks, stones, the wood of a biome) and the check of an own blueprint (name, size, unbreakable blocks, empty, a bed for a house, a container for a warehouse, an unknown role, the limit).

`runGameTestServer` runs the game tests in a separate world (`run-gametest/`) and exits with a non-zero code if any test fails. They include founding through the API, protection (an outsider cannot break blocks or open a chest, a member with the right can), an explosion in kingdom land, claiming and giving up chunks, and read-only mode for data of a newer version and for a damaged format version; a citizen's body comes from its record and leaves without duplicates, death changes the record and writes the chronicle, food is taken from a storehouse, a newcomer arrives with a bed and food, the farmer harvests, replants and stores, hoppers put into and take from a storehouse, citizens and storehouses survive saving, and format 2 data is upgraded without loss; a smelter fills a vanilla furnace with raw metal and fuel and takes out the ingot, a cook cooks food, a crafter makes what was ordered at the crafting table, a smith upgrades to netherite at the smithing table keeping the enchantments and makes an iron sword on order through the chain from raw iron, a reservation keeps two workers off one ingredient, a keep order holds its quota, special recipes are left out, making things far from players neither duplicates nor loses anything with full storehouses, orders and workshops survive saving and format 4 data is upgraded; a minter far from players and near them strikes coins from ingots of the storehouses and leaves metal a keep order holds, plenty of copper does not hold up striking silver from iron, the bank takes and pays out coins without duplicates or losses (also with a full inventory), players pay each other, wages are paid and an empty treasury spoils the mood, the tax is taken and debts grow, the server checks the Treasury right, item values come from tags and recipes, treasuries and accounts survive saving, format 5 data is upgraded, and damaged money makes the data read-only. Trade: a player buys at a stall with coins and from the account, the kingdom buys within its budget, a lot sells with the tax for the treasury and is taken back, merchants sell surplus abroad within the daily limit, goods from abroad arrive after the delay, an exchange keeps the money of both currencies and stays within the treasury, old coins melt into metal, a caravan delivers and pays and survives saving, a lost caravan gives the payment back, the server checks the Market and Deals rights, coins of the vault reach the king, and trade survives saving and the upgrade from format 6. The guild: a monster killed by a player leaves a crystal of its rank, animals and monsters killed without a player leave nothing; monsters from a spawner give less and less; a player joins at a desk kept by a registrar and gets rank F; a hunt counts kills and pays from escrow with the commission for the treasury; a contract that runs out gives the escrow back and costs points; items are handed in all at once or not at all; an exam raises the rank; a squad goes on an expedition without bodies, takes weapons from the storehouse and comes back with crystals; a death on an expedition goes to the chronicle; the server checks the right "Adventurers' guild"; the guild survives saving, data of a newer version stays read-only and the marshal gets the right once. A caravan that waits too long for room closes its deal with the payment and the goods going back. Construction: a builder near players builds a small template from the materials of a storehouse, clearing sends drops to the storehouse and stops at a container with items, bedrock, a spawner, reinforced deepslate and other protected blocks are never broken, whether laying out, far from players or by an operator, nothing is broken or placed on land lost before the place is loaded and what was paid for is built once the land is back, what is missing is shown and ordered from the workshops, cancelling gives the materials back without duplicates, a storehouse and a post of a template register as if a player placed them, the server refuses foreign land, a throne inside the building, overlaps and players without the right, a template with a block of a missing mod, with bedrock or too large cannot be built while the built-in ones can, work far from players pays for blocks, places them when their place is loaded and survives saving, format 7 data is upgraded, and stairs turn the way the core turns directions. Buildings: a house gives homes, comfort and room for newcomers; the containers of a warehouse become storehouses and stop being so when it is damaged; a damaged workshop loses its role, a builder far from players repairs it with materials from the storehouse and the role comes back; an upgrade changes only the difference, leaves a chest with things untouched and pays only for what is new; a demolition gives materials back with the loss and what a chest holds whole, and one cancelled half way leaves the building damaged; an own blueprint without a bed or with bedrock is not saved, a saved one survives saving and is built; a template is built of spruce with a cost in spruce and stairs keeping their facing; a subject without the right changes nothing, and a demolition on lost land breaks nothing; format 8 is upgraded and format 9 keeps buildings, constructions and blueprints. Roads: builders lay a section from the gravel of the storehouse (the turf goes to the storehouse), a road is not laid on foreign land without consent, a player is faster on a road, a caravan takes a road and arrives sooner, an ambush takes goods into the camp and gives the payment back, a player clears a camp and takes its chest (the blocks of the camp drop nothing), a robber breaks no blocks and spares a player away from the keyboard, the server checks rights, roads, camps, walls and remembered consents survive saving; builders put up a palisade of logs from the storehouse around a city on the lay of the land with gates where a road crosses it, the wall and its gates enter the register as fortifications and lose integrity when broken, and a player coming into the city through a gate and past the gates raises the event; the wall plays the role of a wall and work by template is refused for it; an escaped slave runs to the nearest camp, becomes a robber and brings its pack into the loot. Life of cities: a citizen goes to the tavern in the evening, drinks from the storehouse and is in a better mood; at night it sleeps in its own bed and gets up in the morning, freeing the bed; a sleeper does not see a theft, a blow close by wakes it and it sees the offender; the innkeeper sells bread from the storehouse, the money goes to the treasury and none is made from nothing, the tavern sells no diamond; a rumor of a camp points at the real camp (direction, distance, strength), its mark is bought once and appears on the atlas; an ill citizen goes to the infirmary and gets well; a festival takes its price from the treasury and its treat from the storehouse, enters the chronicle, raises the mood and is not repeated the same day. The core unit tests `LifeRulesTest` check the day with its spread, the choice of rumors, the chance of illness and getting well, festivals (mood, price, the day of the founding) and fairs (days and prices), and `TranslationKeysTest` the plural forms of the life of cities.

Unit tests of the army check the strength of fighters (the strength of the guild's adventurers unchanged included), abstract battles (an empty side, even sides, an overwhelming side, the loser suffers more, the same random numbers give the same battle, battles without risk), guards lowering the chance of losing a caravan, the choice of gear by attributes with overrides and bans, recruiting and dismissing with the "Army" right, squads and orders, hiring and unpaid mercenaries leaving without making money, the limit of mercenaries, ranks and the cap of training, and the translation test the plural forms of soldiers, mercenaries, fighters, enemies and squads. Game tests of the army: a recruit takes the best weapon and armor of the storehouse and brings them back when dismissed, a soldier kills a monster on the land, a soldier never attacks a foreign player in peace, an unpaid band leaves and no money appears, guards save a caravan from an ambush, a fallen soldier goes to the chronicle, the server checks the "Army" right, the army survives saving, newer data stays read-only, the marshal gets the right once.

The smoke script installs a real Forge 1.20.1-47.4.10 server into `D:\Projects\kingdom-world-testenv` (outside the repository), adds the built jar, accepts the EULA and starts the server twice on a fresh world. The first session founds the kingdom `SmokeTest` with a throne at 8 70 8 (`kingdomworld admin found`), claims chunk [2, 0], checks that a detached chunk is refused, adds three citizens, kills one by command and checks that two remain and none has a body without players. Then it sets up a smelter post at a furnace and a smithy at a crafting table, stores raw iron, coal and a stick, orders an iron sword and checks that after simulation steps the sword is in the storehouse and a keep order for bread stays; with `-Pack` it also finds a tool or weapon of a mod of the pack with a crafting table recipe, stores its ingredients and checks that the smith makes it on order. Then the treasury gets 1000 from an operator command, a minter far from players strikes 10 silver coins from 5 iron ingots of the storehouse, a payday pays the wages, the worth of every item (of the pack's mods too with `-Pack`) is worked out without errors and an iron sword is valued by its recipe. The second session checks that the kingdom, its id, all 10 chunks, the same citizens, the posts, the sword, the order and the treasury's books, vault and funds survived, with nothing lost or doubled (exactly the same numbers). The script exits with code 1 if the server fails to start or stop, if the logs contain errors or exceptions related to `kingdomworld`, if fire protection is not active, if a client class of the mod is loaded on the server, or if the data does not survive the restart. Logs of each session are kept in `smoke-logs` inside the server folder. Commands are written to the server console as UTF-8 without a BOM, so the script also works under code page 65001. Then a second kingdom, SmokePartner, is founded to the east (640 70 8) and accepts a deal for 32 wheat: the caravan leaves with the payment in escrow, a test buyer buys wheat at the market from the account, and the money of both currencies adds up. The second session checks that the caravan survived the restart on its way, brings it in (`admin trade arrive`) and checks that the wheat is in the buyer's storehouse, the payment in the seller's reserves and that no money appeared. Construction: in the first session the server loads the templates (the built-in ones and the one of the script's data pack that needs Create: with `-Pack` it is built, on the clean server it cannot be built and names the mod), a builder's post and a builder appear on a flattened place of chunk [2, 0], a small house is laid out, its materials go into the storehouse and the builder builds half of it far from players, and the construction is paused before the restart; in the second session the construction is in its place with the same numbers and, let go on, the builder finishes the house, it enters the register and its materials in the storehouse add up with nothing left over. Buildings (chunk [-1, 1]): a well of birch built by an operator plays its role, loses blocks and rests, a builder far from players repairs it with materials from the storehouse and the role comes back, then a demolition removes it from the register and brings birch and stone to the storehouse; a hut that stands in the world is saved as an own blueprint, a construction from it waits paused over the restart with the same numbers, is finished in the second session and enters the register as a house, with the materials adding up. With `-Pack` a small house is built of the first wood of a mod of the pack: its planks are in the cost and the building is whole by its own blocks. The life of cities in the smoke test (chance illnesses and fairs by themselves are off there): on flattened chunk [-1, -1] an operator builds a tavern, it plays its role, a new citizen becomes the innkeeper at the post behind the counter and the tavern is open; the innkeeper tells rumors; a citizen falls ill; a festival takes exactly its price from the treasury and its treat from the storehouse, and the money adds up; the fair of the capital opens. In the second session the life of cities is loaded, the ill citizen is still ill, the tavern is open and a second festival the same day is refused without touching the money.

Unit tests of companions and parties check the split of rewards without losing a coin (on random shares too), the shared progress of a contract of a party, hand-ins in parts and contracts leaving with their holder, invitations, the limit and the lead, the offers of the market (the same for a day, new in a new period) and the price by strength, the limit by rank, buying (the money goes to the treasury, none appears), a closed market and the law, hunger, the warning and the escape, gifts and good keeping, freedom, wages and leaving unpaid, selling from the treasury, experience and levels. Game tests: buying a slave, a companion follows and kills a monster, never hurts a foreign player, the pack survives the owner leaving and writing and reading the data without a copy, a hungry slave warns and runs away, freedom into service and into a kingdom, a party of two splits a reward, friendly fire off, a king closes the market, packets.

Unit tests of the law check the rules of crime (king, official, owner, right, war and nobility as exemptions, subjects answer), thefts and blows adding up to one crime that costs reputation once, the fine of twice a theft with the multiplier of the court, execution and its replacement by a fine, the right of the court and fines that may only be lowered, the pardon, petty records fading with reputation (a murder never), the plan of a fine (coins, account, debt), debts paid back from income without making money, the ransom rounded up, the life of confiscated goods, reputation within its bounds and from trade, its weight on prices and the entry fee, the decision at a gate and the pass for a day, the laws of the court within their bounds; the translation test checks the plural forms of times, crimes, days and minutes. Game tests of the law: a theft from a storehouse seen by a citizen makes the thief wanted once reported, an unseen one does not; a witness killed before the report tells nothing; the king and an official take from the storehouse without crime, a subject without the right does not; a guard beats a thief down (alive, the fine taken from the coins, the money adds up) and a poor thief ends with a debt; an executed murderer loses the goods into confiscation and the coins into the treasury, the ransom of half their worth gives back exactly those goods (the named sword too) and cannot be paid twice; guards leave a player away from the keyboard alone and killing them is no head for a hunter; a pardon clears the record, a thief cannot pardon himself; the contract for a head is done by killing the wanted, the hunter is paid and is no murderer; the ban of slavery frees a foreign slave and fines the owner; the law survives saving, and goods of a mod that is gone keep it read-only.

The law in the smoke test: a thief who is offline is wanted for a theft of 120 and pays the fine of 240 from an account of 100, the remaining 140 becomes a debt, the reputation drops; another player is wanted for a blow; a murderer (a stand-in with a sword, bread and a chestplate) is executed: 18 items in confiscation, nothing with the body, with `-Pack` Jack's Gravestones makes no grave; the money of the kingdom grew exactly by what the operator gave. After the restart the wanted, the confiscated goods, the debt, the reputation and the pass are the same, the money adds up, and the murderer buys exactly the 18 items back at the throne for half their worth.

Companions in the smoke test: a player who is offline gets money on the account and buys a slave (the price goes to the treasury, no money appears), puts iron into the pack and forms a party; with `-Pack` the companion takes a weapon of a mod of the pack over an iron sword. After the restart the companion, its pack, its weapon and the party are the same.

Roads in the smoke test: in the first session the city Smoke Hollow is founded at 40 100 8, the script levels the wild land between the kingdoms (a strip along z = 8 from x 46 to 591, and the land around the city), the operator plans a path along it (`admin roads road plan`, the way searched on the server over ticks), pays for it and places it in the world, a camp of robbers appears far from the way (5 robbers and a leader), `admin roads gear` arms robbers (with `-Pack` with weapons and armor of the pack's mods); the caravan of the deal takes the road (`admin roads caravans`: its way on roads). Caravans are slow in the smoke test (`caravanBlocksPerMinute = 20`), and no camp appears or grows by itself. In the second session the city, the road with the same blocks paid, the camp and the way of the caravan are the same. The world is new every time: if no way is found along the strip (water ran into it, say), the road checks are skipped with a warning. A palisade goes up around Smoke Hollow (`wallRadius = 4` in the smoke test): the ring on the lay of the land with a gate where the road leaves, paid with logs from the storehouses, placed and entered into the register of buildings, and a gate cut by hand on the north side; after the restart the wall is the same. If the city stands in water in this world, the wall checks are skipped with a warning. After the check of the mint the mint goes, so that later steps of the simulation do not move the money between the checks.

Unit tests of the kingdoms of the world check that the plan of places is the same for the same seed, keeps capitals apart and away from the spawn, shifts and skips taken places and goes on from where it stopped; the choice of the size by weights and what a kingdom is made of (a small one stays a village, a medium one is a town, a large one a large city, everyone has a home, no more work than people, plots are taken once, fronts look at the throne); names without repeats in both alphabets; rulers with one or two traits that agree; laws by the character of the ruler; the wealth at the start (the treasury, food for the days asked, gear for every soldier); plural forms. Game tests build a platform at height 290 and check that a small kingdom appears (a throne, land, buildings with roles standing on the ground, citizens with homes and work, soldiers with an order, a treasury that agrees with its "Founding" journal, a palisade), that an appearance leaves the land and buildings of others alone and a place where players spent hours, that a town where players spent hours is left out and the blocks of the player stay, that a restart in the middle and a second appearance double nothing, that the market of a kingdom of the world sells to a player and that a player becomes its subject.

Kingdoms of the world in the smoke test: at the end of the second session the operator calls a large kingdom of the world at -700 -700 (`admin npc spawn`) and the server stops right after it claims its land; the third session checks that the appearance went on, ended without a second founding, the founding money came once (10000) and agrees with the treasury, no citizen came twice, then calls another large kingdom at 700 -700 and measures the ticks of its whole appearance (a worst tick over 50 ms fails; long ticks are logged with their stage). With `-Pack` the same in the biomes of the mods of the pack, with the wood of the place in the log.
Rulers in the smoke test: after the second large kingdom `admin npc days <site> 10` runs ten turns (the treasury agrees with the journal, the ruler lays out a building and trades at its market or in deals); the fourth session after a restart checks the memory of the ruler and format 2, calls six small kingdoms more and measures the ticks of a turn of all eight (`admin npc turn all`): the average tick, the worst one, the worst without pauses of the garbage collector and the worst tick of a turn itself (the look at a kingdom, the decisions, the answers), which must stay within 50 ms; laying out a building goes through the shared path of construction (reservations of all constructions are counted again) and may take longer, which is reported as a warning. Unit tests of decisions from a snapshot are in `RulerMindTest` and `RulerMemoryTest`, game tests of rulers in `RulerGameTests` (a house when the beds are taken and the builders at work, land of others and land where players lived, a deal of a player, money and the journal, a decree in the chronicle).

The army in the smoke test: in the first session two or three citizens become warriors and an archer and take the best weapons of the storehouse (with `-Pack` the best weapons of Simply Swords and of L'Ender's Cataclysm with Cataclysmic Combat, found with `admin army weapons`; the choice is logged), a squad holds the point 20 70 20, a band of mercenaries is hired for its advance from the treasury (the money of the currency falls by exactly the advance). In the second session the squad with its order, the band with the day it is paid until and the soldiers with the same weapons are there, and dismissed soldiers bring exactly one of each of their items back to the storehouse; no money appeared.

Script options:

- `-Pack`: a server in `server-pack` with the server-side and common mods of the modpack (default `D:\Games\Minecraft\Instances\Taghi RPG & Create RU\mods`; the folder is only read). Client-only mods are skipped.
- `-SkipBuild`: use the jar that is already built.
- `-WithoutMod`: diagnostic run without KingdomWorld, to see whether a pack mod breaks the server on its own.
- `-ExcludeModIds a,b`: leave out additional mods by id.
- `-TestEnvRoot`, `-PackModsDir`, `-JavaHome`, `-Memory`: paths and server memory.
- `-MapModsDir <folder>`: also install the server map mods of the folder (BlueMap, Dynmap; other jars are skipped, the folder is only read). The script accepts the download of the game's resources in BlueMap's config (BlueMap does not start without it) and checks after the first start and after the restart that every map mod shows both kingdoms and the caravan: by the marker lines in the log, and for BlueMap also by its live markers over HTTP (`http://127.0.0.1:8100`).

Unit tests of land outlines check a rectangle, the letter L, a hole, enclaves and chunks touching at a corner, neighbouring kingdoms with a shared border, 200 random shapes (the centre of every chunk lies in exactly its area, and outside the land in none) and changes: claiming or losing a chunk redraws only its kingdom, losing a middle chunk leaves a hole, losing a bridge leaves two enclaves. The game test of maps checks that a client gets all land of its dimension, over the limit only the square around the player, that a kingdom hidden from the player reaches neither the snapshot nor the atlas, that the snapshot survives the trip and that the request goes through the request guard.

Unit tests of diplomacy check the bonds and their changes (peace, pact, alliance, vassal, war, truce), the bans on declaring war, the preparation and start of a war with the call of allies and vassals, score and exhaustion within their range, the white peace when the time runs out, terms of peace by the lead and their application together (vassalage, tribute, claims), the independence of a vassal, the shield of the absent (with its delay, not right after a fight), the decisions of rulers of the world by character and the limits on their wars, and plural forms. Game tests of war: a declaration, its preparation and start; soldiers attack an enemy player online and leave one away or offline alone; PvP between kingdoms at war goes through, between kingdoms at peace it does not; a raid takes goods from the cargo of a caravan without making any and pays the buyer back; peace with an indemnity moves money by the books; a kingdom with nobody online is shielded; the law does not judge the killing of an enemy; the data outlives a save. The smoke test declares two wars of kingdoms of the world and a war of the kingdom of players against a kingdom of the world, measures the ticks and steps of the wars, checks score and exhaustion after a restart and makes peace with an indemnity, checking the money.

Unit tests of the nobility check the stability and its causes, how often parties demand (played days only), answers and their consequences (a bargain halves both), a plot that ripens, is found out by counter-intelligence and overthrows a ruler of the world, a plot against a king of players (no overthrow: rebels keep income, after a warning a city leaves), nothing moving while nobody is online, the success, failure and capture of spies, theft from a treasury by the books, marriages of houses. Game tests (`NobilityGameTests`): a title makes a player noble with the share of the fief and no rights over the treasury; a demand and its answer move the stability; a plot changes the ruler of a kingdom of the world; a plot against a king of players takes a city into a kingdom of the world of its own without loss or duplication; a spy steals from a foreign treasury by the books; a caught spy spoils the relations; the nobility outlives a save.

The nobility in the smoke check: in the seventh session `admin nobility tick` makes the courts of the kingdoms of the world, SmokeTest gets a house with the title of count on its east march, a plot, a demand and a spy sent to scout; `admin nobility day` runs the day of the courts one kingdom a tick and prints the average and worst tick; in the eighth session, after the restart, the counts of houses, titles, demands, plots and spies and the money of SmokeTest add up.

### Running in development

- `gradlew runClient`: client with the mod (folder `run/`). `gradlew runClient -PquickPlayWorld=<world folder>` opens a singleplayer world right away.
- `gradlew runServer`: dedicated server with the mod (folder `run/`; accept the EULA in `run/eula.txt`).
- `-PwithMaps=journeymap`, `-PwithMaps=xaero` or `-PwithMaps=all` adds the client map mods to the development runs (downloaded from Modrinth; nothing of them goes into the jar, and the usual build and tests run without them). The JourneyMap API and the library of Xaero's maps are only published inside the mods, so the build takes them from there. The first build needs access to `repo.mikeprimm.com` (Dynmap API), `repo.bluecolored.de` (BlueMap API) and Modrinth.
- `-PwithMagic` adds Iron's Spells 'n Spellbooks and Ars Nouveau (with GeckoLib, Curios, Caelus and playerAnimator) to the development runs; their APIs are only needed at compile time, nothing of them goes into the jar.
- Maps self-check: `gradlew runClient -PwithMaps=journeymap -PclientSelfTest=maps -PquickPlayWorld=<world folder> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` founds two kingdoms in a singleplayer world (a square with a hole and one shaped like the letter L with a Cyrillic name), shows a caravan between them and, once the map mod has drawn the terrain, saves screenshots of the minimap and of the full screen map (with the pointer over the capital of the first kingdom and over the land of the second) in English and Russian, then dissolves the kingdoms and quits. `-PwithMaps=xaero` does the same for Xaero's maps.
- Client self-check: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=title` reaches the title screen and quits; `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=world -PquickPlayWorld=<world folder>` founds a kingdom next to the player in a singleplayer world, opens the map, the throne screen (through the real server packet) with all tabs, the territory editor with a claim, the post screen, the bank screen and the founding screen, then dissolves the kingdom and quits. Steps are logged with the prefix `KingdomWorld client self-test`. Without the `clientSelfTest` property this code does nothing.
- Siege showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=siege -PquickPlayWorld=<copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` puts up a stone wall with a portcullis around a capital next to the player, declares a war of raiders for a city, sends six soldiers with a ram to the gate, marks a second city taken and some chunks occupied, and saves the closed gate, the siege of the gate with the line above the hotbar and the atlas into the folder `world`. Game tests of sieges: the land of an enemy is open in war but not of a third kingdom, not under the shield of the absent and never the throne; a chest is robbed only in war; gates close before enemies, open for their own and break to a soldier with a ram in the expected time; a square is held far from players, defenders interrupt it and the peace hands the city over; an annexation moves the treasury and the citizens without duplicates; builders mend a broken block of a wall from the storehouse after the war; the load of thirty against thirty. Smoke: a kingdom of the world besieges the city of SmokeTest, the square is held 40% over a restart, the peace for the city hands it over, citizens and cities add up.
- Layout check: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=layout -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` draws every screen of the mod off-screen in English and Russian, for windows of 854x480 and 1920x1080 and GUI scales 2, 3 and 4 (clamped as the game clamps them), and checks that texts fit their place and their buttons, nothing is cut off or leaves the screen, and lines above the hotbar fit the narrowest screen. Problems go to the log (language, window, scale, screen, translation key) and to `layout-check.txt`, and the client exits with code 1. Screenshots of scales 2 and 4 are saved to the folder. The screens size themselves from the width of the translated texts; long kingdom and player names are shortened with an ellipsis and shown in full on hover.
- Roads showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=roads -PquickPlayWorld=<copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` flattens land with a stream next to the player, founds a kingdom with a storehouse of gravel and planks, lays a gravel road across the stream (with a bridge) paid from the storehouse, puts a merchant with llamas on the road and a camp of robbers beside it, then takes screenshots of the bridge, the caravan and the camp into the folder `world`.
- Kingdom of the world showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=npc -PquickPlayWorld=<copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` finds flat land near the player, calls a large kingdom of the world there (it appears as for any player, over ticks, with towns and roads), flies over it and saves screenshots of the capital, the square, the wall, a gate, the market, the road to a town, the town and the atlas into the folder `world`.
- Life showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=life -PquickPlayWorld=<a copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` builds the tavern of the mod next to the player with an innkeeper, guests and food in a storehouse and three beds beside it, and in the evening shoots the guests in the tavern and the window of the tavern, at night the citizens asleep, into the folder `world`.

### Commands

For every player (`/kingdom`):

- `info [name]`: your kingdom, the kingdom whose land you stand on, or the named one.
- `list`: all kingdoms.
- `join <name>`: apply; `accept [name]` and `decline <name>`: answer an invitation.
- `leave`: leave your kingdom.
- `invite <player>`, `approve <player>`, `reject <player>`, `kick <player>`: for the recruiting right.
- `role <player> <position>`: appoint (`marshal`, `treasurer`, `chancellor`, `spymaster`, `court_mage`, `steward`, `subject`), king only.
- `abdicate <player>`: hand over the crown.
- `claim`, `unclaim`: claim or give up the chunk you stand in (territory right).
- `citizens`: the citizens of your kingdom (or of the land you stand on): population, beds, food, mood.
- `balance`: your accounts in every currency and your debts to treasuries.
- `pay <player> <amount> [kingdom]`, `pay confirm`: pay another player in the currency of your kingdom or the named one; large payments must be confirmed.
- `treasury`: the treasury of your kingdom; `treasury pay <player> <amount>`: pay from the treasury (Treasury right); `treasury deposit <amount> [kingdom]`: pay from your account into a treasury (debts first).
- `value [item]`: the worth of the item in your hand or the named one.
- `dissolve`, then `dissolve confirm` within 30 seconds: dissolve the kingdom (king only).

Operators (permission level 2, `/kingdomworld`):

- `admin found <name> <x y z> [ruler]`: place a throne and found a kingdom (without a ruler from the console).
- `admin delete <name>`: delete a kingdom.
- `admin setruler <name> <player>`: set the ruler.
- `admin claim|unclaim <name> <chunkX> <chunkZ>`: claim or give up a chunk without rights or limit checks (the other rules apply).
- `admin owner <chunkX> <chunkZ>`: who owns a chunk.
- `admin bypass`: toggle protection bypass.
- `admin data status`: state of the data; `admin data unlock`: leave read-only mode (a backup is made first, then only what could be read is kept).
- `admin citizens list [kingdom]`: citizens (short id, name, profession, state, health, home) and how many are in the world now.
- `admin citizens spawn <count> [kingdom]`: add citizens regardless of the growth rules (next to you, or at the throne from the console).
- `admin citizens kill <id>`: the citizen dies (with a chronicle entry); `admin citizens info <id>`: everything about a citizen; `admin citizens profession <id> <profession>`: give a profession. The start of the id is enough.
- `admin posts list [kingdom]`: work posts (place, profession, workers, radius, state, gathered today, yield); `admin posts set <x y z> profession|radius|seats|herd|animal|auto <value>`: set up a post without checking rights.
- `admin orders list [kingdom]`: orders with their sub-orders and what is missing; `admin orders add <item> <count> keep|make [kingdom]`: give an order; `admin orders cancel <number> [kingdom]`: cancel it.
- `admin recipes list <profession> [filter]`: what a workshop makes (the filter `mods` keeps only items of mods, any other filter matches part of the id); `admin recipes stock <item> [kingdom]`: put the ingredients of one making into the storehouses.
- `admin storage put <item> <count> [kingdom]`: put items into the storehouses; `admin storage list [kingdom]`: what the storehouses hold.
- `admin economy give|take <player> <amount> [kingdom]`: add or take money on an account; `admin economy treasury <kingdom> add|take <amount>`: change a treasury (journaled as admin); `admin economy payday <kingdom>`: hold the payday now; `admin economy info <kingdom>`: the treasury with exact numbers; `admin economy accounts <player>`: the accounts of a player; `admin economy values`: work out item values again and show the totals; `admin economy value <item>`: the worth of an item.
- `admin trade sell <kingdom> <item> <price> <keep>`, `admin trade buy <kingdom> <item> <price> <up to> <budget>`, `admin trade export <kingdom> <item> <above>`, `admin trade import <kingdom> <item> <count>`: the market of a kingdom (a price of 0 is the auto price, a budget of 0 has no limit); `admin trade propose <seller> <buyer> <item> <count> <price> [days]`: propose a deal for the seller (the price in the buyer's currency), `admin trade accept <kingdom> <number>`: accept it; `admin trade deals`, `admin trade caravans`: deals and caravans with exact numbers; `admin trade arrive`: every caravan arrives now without the chance of loss; `admin trade wallet <kingdom> <name> <amount>` and `admin trade purchase <kingdom> <name> <item> <count>`: money for a test buyer and a purchase at the market from its account; `admin trade rates`: rates, the money of currencies, coins in hands, treasuries and reserves.
- `admin guild rank <player> <rank>`, `admin guild points <player> <amount>`, `admin guild info <player>`, `admin guild join <player> <kingdom>`: rank, points and membership in the guild; `admin guild crystal <player> <rank> [count]`: give crystals; `admin guild contract list [kingdom]`, `admin guild contract post <kingdom> hunt|gather|bounty <monster, item or rank> <count> <reward> [days]` (ids with a colon in quotes: `"minecraft:zombie"`), `admin guild contract take <player> <id>`, `admin guild contract kill <player> <monster> [count]`, `admin guild contract expire <id>`, `admin guild contract cancel <id>`: contracts (the kingdom posts them from its treasury); `admin guild hire <kingdom> <count>`: citizens without work become adventurers; `admin guild squad list [kingdom]`, `admin guild squad form <kingdom> <size>`, `admin guild squad send <squad> <contract>`, `admin guild squad return`: squads and expeditions (bringing them back at once); `admin guild monsters [filter]`: ranks of the monsters of the game and the mods; `admin guild money`: money in the guild's escrow; `admin guild energy <kingdom>`: crystals and energy; `admin guild status`, `admin guild unlock`: the state of the guild's data and leaving read-only mode.
- `admin build place <kingdom> <template> <x y z> <turn> <mirror> <wood> [stone]`: a construction of other wood and stone (`"minecraft:spruce"`, `auto` for the wood of the place, `none` as drawn); `admin build priority <number> low|normal|high`: the priority of a construction; `admin build materials`: the woods and stones of the server; `admin build role <building>`: role, its state, level, integrity, beds and residents, numbers, materials, costs of an upgrade and a repair; `admin build upgrade|repair|demolish <building>`: order the work; `admin build autorepair <building> true|false`; `admin build check <building>`: look at the building now; `admin build damage <building> <blocks>`: take blocks away from the top without drops (for checks); `admin build blueprints [kingdom]`: own blueprints; `admin build capture <kingdom> <x y z> <x y z> <name> [role] [category]`: save a building as a blueprint.
- `admin build templates`: building templates, their size, blocks and items and whether they can be built; `admin build list [kingdom]`, `admin build buildings [kingdom]`: constructions and the register with exact numbers; `admin build place <kingdom> <template> <x y z> [rotation 0-3] [mirror]`: lay out a construction; `admin build info <id>`: steps, placed and paid blocks, what is still to pay, what lies at the site and what is set aside; `admin build stock <id>`: put what a construction still needs into the storehouses; `admin build finish <id>`: build it at once without materials (the register remembers the operator); `admin build cancel <id>`: cancel it; `admin build pause <id>`, `admin build resume <id>`: pause it and let it go on; `admin build tick`: place paid blocks and refresh states now.
- `admin army status`, `admin army unlock`: the state of the army's data and leaving read-only mode; `admin army recruit <kingdom> <count> [warrior|archer]`: unemployed citizens become soldiers and take their gear; `admin army dismiss <kingdom>`: dismiss every soldier; `admin army soldiers <kingdom>` and `admin army gear <kingdom>`: soldiers with their weapons and armor (the second refreshes the gear first); `admin army weapons <mod>`: the weapons of a mod as soldiers judge them; `admin army squad list [kingdom]`, `admin army squad form <kingdom> <size>`, `admin army squad disband <squad>`: squads; `admin army order <squad> none|hold <x y z>|patrol <x y z>|guard <x y z> <radius>|follow <player>|escort <deal>`: orders; `admin army mercenary offers <kingdom> [new]`, `admin army mercenary hire <kingdom> <band>`, `admin army mercenary release <band>`, `admin army mercenary list [kingdom]`: mercenaries; `admin army battle <squad> <monsters> [strength]`: an abstract battle for checks.
- `admin companions status`, `admin companions unlock`: the state of the companion data and leaving read-only mode; `admin companions offer <kingdom> [refresh]`: the offers of a market (new ones at once); `admin companions give <player> [level] [kingdom]`, `fund <player> <kingdom> <amount>`, `buy <player> <kingdom>`: a companion, money on the account, buying the first slave by the rules of the market; `pack <player> <item> <count>`, `gear <player>`, `list <player>`, `loyalty <player> <0-100>`, `hunger <player> <0-20>`: the pack, the gear, the list, loyalty and food of companions; `market <kingdom> <true|false>`: close or open the slave market; `party add <leader> <member>`, `party fire <leader> <true|false>`, `party disband <leader>`: parties. A player is given by UUID or by name, offline too.
- `admin law status`, `admin law unlock`: the state of the law data and leaving read-only mode; `admin law wanted [kingdom]`: the wanted; `admin law wanted add <player> <kingdom> theft|assault|murder|trespass [worth]`: a crime seen by guards; `admin law report`: deliver the reports now; `admin law pardon <player> <kingdom>`; `admin law fine <player> <kingdom> pay|set <amount>`: take the fine (coins, account, debt) or set it; `admin law execute <player> <kingdom> [kit]`: an execution (a player who is offline is replaced by a stand-in at the throne, `kit` gives it a sword, bread and a chestplate); `admin law confiscated [player]`, `confiscated ransom <id>`, `confiscated store <id>`: confiscated goods, the ransom from the owner's account at the throne, sending them to the storehouses; `admin law reputation <player> <kingdom> [-100..100]`; `admin law debt <player> <kingdom> [add <amount>]`; `admin law pass <player> <kingdom> [grant|revoke]`; `admin law gate <player> <kingdom>`: a player online comes to the gate of a large city; `admin law court <kingdom> [multiplier|fee|bounty|slavery <value>]`: the laws of the court. A player is given by UUID or name, also when offline.
- `/kingdom law`: the window of the law.
- `/kingdom party`: the party window; `/kingdom party invite|kick|leader <player>`, `accept`, `decline`, `leave`, `fire <true|false>`, `say <text>`; `/party <text>`: party chat.
- `admin magic energy <kingdom> [add|set <amount>]`, `admin magic crystals <kingdom> <rank> <count>`, `admin magic dissolve <kingdom> <count>`: the energy of a kingdom (store, capacity, crystals, research, spells, devices), crystals into the storehouses and dissolving the crystals of the storehouses; `admin magic research <kingdom> <research> done|undo|start`, `admin magic spell <kingdom> <spell> on|off`, `admin magic ward <kingdom> <chunkX> <chunkZ>`: the tower and the spells of the kingdom; `admin magic learn <player> <spell> [forget]`, `admin magic staff <player>`, `admin magic cast <player> <spell>`: magic of players; `admin magic wells <kingdom> <pos>`: a pulse of the wells of other mods with a stand-in player, totals to the log; `admin magic status|unlock`: the state of the magic data.
- `admin roads status`, `admin roads unlock`: the state of the road data and leaving read-only mode; `admin roads city list`, `admin roads city add <kingdom> <x y z> [name]` (places a town hall), `admin roads city remove <id>`, `admin roads city level <kingdom> <city|0>` (level, citizens, buildings, gates): cities; `admin roads wall list`, `admin roads wall build <kingdom> <city|0> palisade|stone`, `admin roads wall gate <id> <x y z>`, `admin roads wall pay <id>`, `admin roads wall place <id>`, `admin roads wall remove <id>`: walls; `admin roads road list`, `admin roads road plan <kingdom> <from x y z> <to x y z> path|gravel|paved [joint|payer]` (searches the way at once), `admin roads road pay <id>` (pay for all of it from the storehouses of its builders), `admin roads road place <id>` (place the paid blocks, loading their chunks), `admin roads road wear <id> <0-1>`, `admin roads road answer <request> accept|decline`, `admin roads road remove <id>`: roads; `admin roads camp list`, `admin roads camp add <x y z> [robbers]`, `admin roads camp grow <id>`, `admin roads camp remove <id>`: camps; `admin roads ambush <caravan>`: the nearest camp falls upon a caravan now; `admin roads caravans`: caravans with their way through the roads; `admin roads gear`: the weapons and armor robbers take.
- `admin npc status`, `admin npc unlock`: the state of the data of the kingdoms of the world and leaving read-only mode; `admin npc plan`: the plan (places, how many wait for players, whether it is made); `admin npc list`: every place with its size, towns, state, stage, kingdom and ruler, and for a kingdom that stands its treasury, founding money, citizens, soldiers, buildings and towns; `admin npc spawn here [small|medium|large]`, `admin npc spawn <x> <z> [size]`: a kingdom of the world appears on flat land near there (within 256 blocks) for checks; `admin npc spawn site <id>`: a planned place appears now; `admin npc remove <id> [buildings]`: the kingdom of a place goes with its land, citizens and treasury, with `buildings` also its buildings, throne, town halls and wall; `admin npc reset-plan`: the places still waiting are forgotten and the plan is made again; `admin npc timing`: the ticks of the last appearance (count, average and worst).
- `admin life status`, `admin life unlock`: the time of day, the ill, rooms, goods of fairs and leaving read-only mode; `admin life time morning|day|evening|night`: set the time to the middle of that part of the day of citizens; `admin life tavern <kingdom>`: taverns, whether open, the guests of the evening; `admin life rumors <kingdom> [refresh]`: the rumors of the taverns today (with the number of a camp); `admin life sick list <kingdom>`, `admin life sick <citizen> [heal]`: illnesses; `admin life festival <kingdom>`: a festival as the king calls it; `admin life fair <kingdom> [city]`: open a fair now.
- `admin war status`, `admin war unlock`: the state of the diplomacy data and leaving read-only mode; `admin war list`: wars (score, exhaustion, battles, raids, blockades, losses, shields) and treaties; `admin war declare <attacker> <defender> <goal> [break|now]`: a war by an operator (the defender may have nobody online, `break` breaks a treaty, `now` starts at once); `admin war start <war>`; `admin war peace <war> [winner [indemnity]]`: a white peace, the goal of the winner in full or an indemnity, money checked; `admin war score <war> [attacker|defender <0-100>]`, `admin war exhaustion <war> attacker|defender <0-100>`; `admin war relation <first> <second> [reason]`; `admin war truce <first> <second> <days>`, `admin war treaty <first> <second> pact|alliance|vassal|truce`; `admin war present <kingdom> on|off|auto`: count the players of a kingdom as online or not (for checks of the shield); `admin war tick [steps]`: steps of the wars now with their timing, `admin war tick day`: a day of the wars now; `admin war timing`: ticks of diplomacy, average and worst.
- `admin npc turn <site>`: a turn of the ruler now (decisions, answers to deals, needs); `admin npc turn all`: a turn of every ruler in turn with the ticks measured; `admin npc days <site> <n>`: n turns in a row with their decisions carried out (the treasury before and after, checked against the journal); `admin npc decisions <site>`: decrees and decisions waiting; `admin npc needs <site>`: needs, relations to neighbours and the times of turns. `admin npc timing` shows the ticks of rulers too.
- `admin simulate <kingdom> [steps]`: run steps of the citizen simulation at once (for tests).
- `debug create|list|info|remove`: the stage 0 debug commands.

Quote kingdom names with spaces in admin commands: `"Old Valoria"`.

### Configuration

- `<world>/serverconfig/kingdomworld-server.toml` (per world):
  - `npc`: `maxActiveNpcs` (300, citizen bodies per server), `citizenActivationChunks` (6);
  - `guild`: `healthWeight` (1), `damageWeight` (4), `armorWeight` (2), `speedWeight` (20), `monsterRankScores` (0, 25, 45, 70, 120, 250, 500, 1000, 2000), `bossRankBonus` (2), `bossMinRank` (`a`), `monsterRanks`, `crystalEnergy` (10 ... 100000), `crystalChance` (0.05 ... 1), `crystalValue` (2 ... 20000), `farmWindowMinutes` (10), `farmFreeKills` (12), `farmHalfKills` (8), `spawnerFactor` (0.5), `rankPoints` (0, 30, 100, 300, 800, 2000, 5000, 12000, 30000), `killPoints` (1 ... 256), `examKills` (5, 5, 4, 3, 1, ...), `examMinutes` (60), `examCooldownMinutes` (20), `penaltyPercent` (50), `joinFee` (0), `maxActiveContracts` (3), `maxContractsPerBranch` (32), `maxPostedPerPlayer` (5), `minCommissionPercent` (0), `maxCommissionPercent` (30), `defaultCommissionPercent` (10), `postingFeePermille` (20), `maxContractDays` (7), `defaultDailyBudget` (50), `maxDailyBudget` (100000), `autoRewardPerKill` (1 ... 1500), `maxAutoContracts` (3), `minSquadSize` (2), `maxSquadSize` (5), `expeditionBaseMinutes` (10), `expeditionMinutesPerRank` (5), `expeditionInjury` (0.25), `expeditionDeath` (0.04); tables by rank hold nine numbers for F...SSS;
  - `law`: `witnessRadius` (16), `reportMinSeconds` (10), `reportMaxSeconds` (30), `pettyDays` (3), `theftFinePercent` (200), `assaultFine` (50), `trespassFine` (30), `murderFine` (1000), `mergeSeconds` (60), `bountyReward` (200), `bountyDays` (3), `ransomPercent` (50), `confiscationDays` (7), `debtLimit` (500), `hailSeconds` (5), `beatenHealth` (2), `beatenWeaknessSeconds` (10), `theftReputation` (-5), `assaultReputation` (-8), `murderReputation` (-40), `trespassReputation` (-3), `tradePerPoint` (500), `taxReputation` (1), `contractReputation` (2), `highReputation` (50), `priceEffectPercent` (10), `slaveryFine` (300), `maxEntryFee` (10000), `maxBounty` (1000000), `slaveryWarnBlocks` (32);
  - `simulation`: `simulationIntervalTicks` (1200, the step of the abstract citizen simulation);
  - `citizens`: `growthFoodDays` (3), `growthIntervalMinutes` (10), `starvationCanKill` (`false`), `dailyNutrition` (6), `infirmaryCapacity` (4), `woundedHealthPercent` (50), `maxCitizensPerKingdom` (200), `farmRadius` (8);
  - `work`: `workPostDefaultRadius` (8), `workPostMaxRadius` (16), `workPostMaxSeats` (4), `maxTreeBlocks` (160), `mineLevels` (4), `maxHerdSize` (12), `carrierRange` (48 blocks), `carrierItemsPerMinute` (64), `taskRiskMultiplier` (1.0; 0 turns accidents far from players off), `professionRisks` (risk per minute of work as `profession:injury:death`, for example `miner:0.004:0.0004`);
  - `production`: `craftSeconds` (10, hand work on one thing for a beginner), `cookFoodDaysTarget` (5), `orderChainDepth` (3), `maxOrdersPerKingdom` (32);
